Chair Debbie Cragun called the meeting to order at 6:31 p.m.
PLANNING COMMISSION BUSINESS MEETING
1.

Invocation and Pledge.

Stephen Walston offered the invocation. Ulises Flynn led the Pledge of Allegiance.
2.

Public Comment.

There were no public comments.
3.

Approval of Minutes from the November 6, 2019, Meeting of the Planning Commission

Stephen Walston moved to approve the minutes from the November 6, 2019, meeting of the
Debbie Cragun seconded the motion. Vote on the motion: Traci

Legislative Items
4.

PUBLIC HEARING, CONSIDERATION, AND RECOMMENDATION on an
Amendment to the City of Bluffdale General Plan, Specifically an Update to the CityWide Transportation Plan. City of Bluffdale, Applicant. Trevor Andra, Staff
Presenter.

Assistant City Engineer, Trevor Andra presented the staff report and stated that work has taken
place to update master plans for transportation, drinking water, stormwater, and parks in

1

DRAFT – FOR DISCUSSION PURPOSES ONLY
BLUFFDALE CITY PLANNING COMMISSION
MEETING MINUTES
Wednesday, December 4, 2019
1

anticipation of an update to impact fees. Mr. Andra noted that the Transportation Plan is part of the

2

General Plan, hence the need for the presentation he was making.

3
4

City Planner/Economic Development Director, Grant Crowell provided background information on

5

the proposed updates to the Transportation Plan. He explained that a comprehensive rewrite of the

6

Transportation Plan took place in 2017. That process included extensive solicitation of public

7

input. The changes being proposed are embedded in that original document.

8
9

Mr. Andra noted that one major factor that affected the Bluffdale Transportation Plan and the

10

update to the Impact Fee Plan was the 2050 Regional Transportation Plan that was recently released

11

by the Wasatch Front Regional Council (WFRC).

12

transportation needs along the Wasatch Front for the years 2030 and 2050. In addition, some

13

projects were completed in Bluffdale and there were pending projects to be completed.

14
15

The staff report included updates on the upcoming projects as well as those that were completed. In

16

response to Chair Cragun’s question regarding whether the numbers assigned to the project

17

represented the priority number for the project, Mr. Andra stated that it was just a list; however, the

18

document itself included general years. Completion of the projects will depend on development and

19

funding. Mr. Crowell explained that a short-term list is used to determine what to include in the

20

annual budget, so the items on the list are not necessarily in rank order. The determining factor is

21

the need for the project. Mr. Andra reviewed the status of projects set forth in the staff report.

22
23

Mr. Andra next noted that there was a list of items that were updated in the report, including

24

feedback from City Council Members, future analysis methodology, growth projections and travel

25

demand models. He clarified that the entire Master Plan was impacted by the growth projections,

26

and based on the term “units” rather than “population.” The report includes the current number of

27

units, along with the projected number of units for 2030 and 2050. In response to Commissioner

28

Crockett’s question regarding whether the last plan was based on units, Mr. Andra said it was based

29

on the Wasatch Front Regional Council’s (“WFRC”) population projections. He liked the current

30

model better because it seemed to provide more accurate data and could be used uniformly across

31

the entire Master Plan.
2

The plan targets anticipated growth and

DRAFT – FOR DISCUSSION PURPOSES ONLY
BLUFFDALE CITY PLANNING COMMISSION
MEETING MINUTES
Wednesday, December 4, 2019
1
2

In response to Commissioner Walston’s question regarding the correlation between the unit growth

3

and population growth, Mr. Andra stated that it was close but it was difficult to use a per-capita

4

count because it is based on the census and is not necessarily accurate. Mr. Crowell added that

5

there are several ways to make projections. One of the City Council’s concerns during the last

6

review of the impact fee was that there were different models used for projecting growth. The new

7

approach will provide for consistency throughout the planning process.

8
9

Mr. Crowell noted that when the City prepares and submits the required reporting for the State

10

Housing Plan, the State requires the City to use a different source of data, which would not be

11

identical to the data used for the City’s General Plan. Mr. Crowell explained that the element of

12

making assumptions is necessary, but it can be tricky to come up with precise figures for the

13

different types of property uses and accompanying needs for water and transportation. Mr. Andra

14

explained that the approach the City was proposing to use was very defensible in projecting growth

15

for 2030 and 2050.

16
17

Mr. Andra noted that the report included projections for 2030 and 2050, a project list, and roadway

18

types based on the projected figures. The report contained existing development areas and unit

19

counts, which were broken down to a parcel level based on the zoning. Thus, there were residential

20

and non-residential units, and the accompanying acreage and land uses. There were efforts to look

21

at areas where growth might take place and would be compatible with the projected growth rate for

22

2030 and 2050. Mr. Crowell interjected that the City Council has looked at how the gravel pit areas

23

might be developed in the future. That planning requires extensive visioning and the numbers have

24

not been included in the 2030 scenario.

25
26

In response to Commissioner Walston’s question regarding the saturation point or build-out of

27

Bluffdale, Mr. Crowell stated that by 2030 the City will have a better idea, with the exception of the

28

gravel pits because they will take longer to develop. Mr. Crowell explained that building up instead

29

of out will affect water use. Mr. Andra stated that 2050 was the anticipated build-out time frame.

30

Mr. Crowell stated that the development at the parcel level was somewhat helpful. Within the next

31

year or so, he expected the City to start looking at the Land Use General Plan, which will provide an
3

DRAFT – FOR DISCUSSION PURPOSES ONLY
BLUFFDALE CITY PLANNING COMMISSION
MEETING MINUTES
Wednesday, December 4, 2019
1

opportunity to verify whether the growth assumptions are as anticipated. Mr. Andra referred to the

2

2050 map, which showed the projected development and unit counts. He clarified that the unit

3

count in the Geneva property was just a projection and was not definitive.

4
5

In response to Commissioner Walston’s question regarding the possibility that the Federal

6

Government will turn over some property to the City, Mr. Crowell stated that development of Camp

7

Williams was not likely at this point because of the expanded use taking place on the site.

8
9

Mr. Andra next reviewed Table 1: Bluffdale 2030 Project List. He briefly described the different

10

projects enumerated. In response to Commissioner Crockett’s question regarding the construction

11

project currently taking place from 14600 South to Redwood Road, Mr. Andra said that the road

12

was three lanes. Eventually, the entire road is slated to be three lanes. With regard to the trestle

13

project, Mr. Crowell clarified that it will be a City project and not a UDOT project. He noted that

14

there are a lot of complexities associated with it. He stressed the importance of focusing on how

15

wide the road could be in the future. The traffic models provide insight into how planning could

16

occur.

17
18

Chair Cragun noted that the projects numbered 1,2, and 5 all pertain to the same space and he had

19

questions with those projects. Mr. Andra explained that Project 5 took into account the full length

20

of 14600 South, whereas Projects 1 and 2 were broken into two sections. The bike lanes along with

21

the curb and sidewalk need to be constructed now, which is why they are higher on the list. The

22

City currently has a three-lane right-of-way all along 14600 South. To expand the right-of-way

23

would require the acquisition of additional property. That process would be costly and time-

24

consuming. Expansion of the road to five lanes would have to be incremental. Mr. Crowell said

25

that 14600 South has a variety of aesthetic needs that should be addressed since it is a major

26

gateway to Bluffdale. The City has been successful with the acquisition of grant funding.

27
28

Chair Cragun requested that Mr. Andra provide a simplified explanation of any major impacts being

29

proposed in the plan. Mr. Andra explained said that the major change was the methodology for

30

projecting future growth using units as opposed to census counts. He noted that the Impact Fee

4

DRAFT – FOR DISCUSSION PURPOSES ONLY
BLUFFDALE CITY PLANNING COMMISSION
MEETING MINUTES
Wednesday, December 4, 2019
1

Plan was also updated, which occurs annually. Mr. Crowell clarified that the only idea on the list

2

that was completely new was item 7. The other items have been on the plan for some time.

3
4

In response to Commissioner Walston regarding the references to canals and walking paths into

5

Herriman, Mr. Crowell stated they were discussed extensively in 2017. Commissioner Walston did

6

not see the value of a walking path across the canal into Herriman. Mr. Crowell said that the

7

prevailing opinion was that it was not a good idea to have roads connect into Herriman to handle the

8

traffic because the Bluffdale infrastructure is not prepared to handle the resulting traffic load.

9

Nevertheless, in 2017 the consensus was that having pedestrian connections would be acceptable

10

because of the need to facilitate connections between the two cities without including vehicular

11

connections. Commissioner Walston opined that if pedestrian traffic was enhanced, there would be

12

more people using the canal road in spite of the restrictions placed on the road by the canal

13

company. It was noted that Herriman wants a connection.

14
15

Commissioner Crockett observed that 13800 South in Herriman is poised for development.

16

Mr. Crowell explained that Bluffdale is not trying to plan a project there. Commissioner Crockett

17

stated that there are roads that are owned by Bluffdale City, some that are owned by UDOT, and

18

some that are private. Mr. Andra stated that the planning taking place includes the minor and major

19

collector roads, along with arterial roads. The private roads fall under the purview of the residential

20

areas through a private developer. Some of the private roads may merit being changed to City

21

roads, but the current level of planning does not focus on that issue.

22
23

Chair Cragun asked what would happen if opinions changed about making road connections from

24

neighboring cities into Bluffdale. Mr. Crowell explained that citizens would want to try to persuade

25

their elected official to change the Transportation Plan. The prevailing feeling was still opposition

26

to making the connection. Chair Cragun stated that people may change their minds when they see

27

that there are things in the neighboring cities they would like to access more easily. Mr. Crowell

28

stated that Bluffdale has unique conditions to deal with such as topography, the river, seven

29

irrigation canals, and the railroad.

30

5

DRAFT – FOR DISCUSSION PURPOSES ONLY

1

Chair Cragun opened the public hearing. There were no public comments. Chair Cragun closed the

2

public hearing.

3
4

Commissioner Walston felt that Planning Commission should forward a positive recommendation

5

to the City Council.

6
7

Stephen Walston moved to forward a positive recommendation to the City Council to accept

8

the amendments to the City of Bluffdale General Plan by adopting the City of Bluffdale

9

Transportation plan as an element of the General Plan. Debbie Cragun seconded the motion.

10

Vote on the motion: Traci Crockett-Aye; Stephen Walston-Aye; Debbie Cragun-Aye. The

11

motion passed unanimously.

Discussion Items
5.

City Council Reports.

Mr. Crowell reported that on November 13, an Economic Development meeting took place with the

18

City Council and some of the members of the Planning Commission. Another joint meeting is

19

scheduled for December 11. He hoped that the City video will be done by that meeting and some

20

visionary renderings of options for the gravel pits. There will also be discussion on things the City

21

Council should focus on at their January 2020 retreat.

22
23

Mr. Crowell stated that the zone change request for the Huskie’z landscaping property was

24

postponed until 2020. It has not been withdrawn, however. The lot coverage was approved. The

25

City Council continued to work on the possibility of changing the parking requirements for when

26

snowstorms occur. It was noted that the State Housing Requirement Report was completed by

27

Senior Planner, Caitlyn Miller.

Mr. Crowell reported that a project is being reviewed for the Woodbury property that includes a

6

7

Site plans for the Bringhurst Station project were imminent. The Saddleback Building on 2700

8

9

was still waiting for the right tenants. There was still no indication of a grocery store wishing to

10

DRC STAFF REPORT
02 January 2020

To:
City of Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Jennifer Robison, Senior Planner, on behalf of the DRC
Re: Plat Amendment Application – Independence at the Point Plat M-3 Amended
Application No:
2019-51
Applicant(s):
Kody McGregor
Project Location:
15416 South Revolutionary Way
Acreage:
.27 acres
Request:
To adjust lot lines of Lot 782 and Parcels A and B owned by the City as
presented.
SUMMARY
The Independence at the Point Plat M-3 was approved by the City Council and was recorded on September
20, 2018. Mr. McGregor is the current owner of lot 782 and is requesting the south and rear property lines
be adjusted with the adjacent property owned by the City. The applicant desires to have more usable area
in the rear of the home for their enjoyment of the property.
The applicant met with the DRC to propose the plat amendment. The overall development was required to
provide a certain amount of open space which includes Parcels A and B within this plat. For the lot lines to
be adjusted and to not affect the overall open space requirement, the adjustment of property with the City
property is equal in providing 496 square feet to either Mr. McGregor or the City (see McGregor/Bluffdale
City Land Swap exhibit). The five-foot public utility/drainage easement will be maintained on the new lot
boundary as shown. The Staff does not have any other concerns and determines the adjustment to be
positive for the development of the open space.
DRC STAFF RECOMMENDATION FOR INDEPENDENCE AT THE POINT PLAT M-3 AMENDED PLAT
APPLICATION:
DRC Staff recommends that the Planning Commission approve the Independence at the Point Plat M-3
Amended Plat Application subject to the following conditions:
1.
2.
3.

That all requirements of the City Codes, adopted ordinances are met and adhered to.
That the lot line adjustment provides 496 square feet to each property owner and does not affect the
overall open space requirement of the Independence at the Point Development Agreement.
That the five -foot public utility/drainage easement be maintained on the adjusted lot 782.

Plat Amendment Application – Kody McGregor, Applicant
January 8, 2020, Planning Commission Meeting
Page 1

4.

That the applicant is responsible for all fees in preparation of the final mylar copy and recording of
the amended plat as approved.

MODEL MOTIONS
Motion for Approval – “I move we approve the Plat Amendment Application 2019-51 subject to the
conditions and based on the findings presented in the staff report dated January 2, 2020 (or as modified by
the conditions below):”
1.

DRC STAFF REPORT
02 January 2020

To:
City of Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Jennifer Robison, Senior Planner, on behalf of the DRC
Re: Bringhurst Station – Site Plan Application for Townhome Project
Application No:
2019-49
Applicant(s):
Edge Homes
Project Location:
Approximately 16201 South Camp Williams Road
General Plan:
Mixed Use and Very Low Density Residential
Zoning:
SD-X Bringhurst Station Project Zone
Acreage:
35.52 acres for Phase 1
Units:
164 Townhome units
Request:
Approval of the Site Plan Application for the townhomes in the Bringhurst
Station project.
SUMMARY
Subject to the DRC staff’s recommendations, the proposed application meets the City requirements for
Site Plan approval as outlined in the Bluffdale City Codes, the SD-X Bringhurst Station Project zoning
district ordinance, and the Bringhurst Station Project Plan (PP) as approved by the City Council on January
9, 2019. The final plats for phase 1 of the project including the 164 townhomes was approved by the City
Council on August 28, 2019.
ANALYSIS
Anticipated Uses, Layout and Access. The site plan provides for 164 townhome units. The townhome units
are all front-loaded units accessed from a 35’ private street and driveways are also required to be a
minimum of 20 feet.
Adequate Public Facilities and Infrastructure. All adequate public facilities will be constructed and
installed with each phase of the project complying will all requirements or conditions of approval for the
final plats.
ROW, Open Space and Landscaping. All park strips and open space areas are required to be installed and
maintained by the Homeowners Association (HOA) for the overall project. Details for park strips,
landscaping and open space amenities and designs are provided with this application.

Site Plan Application – Bringhurst Station – Edge Homes
January 8, 2020, Planning Commission Meeting
Page 1

Architectural and Design Committee. The SD-X Bringhurst Station Project Zone provides for a design
review committee for all dwelling units and other buildings within the Bringhurst project. The architectural
designs and details for all dwelling types including building materials, colors, elevations, fencing, open
space has been submitted for review and approved by the Bringhurst Station Architectural Review
Committee (BSARC). A letter with approval of the BSARC shall be submitted with a building permit
application.
DRC REVIEW AND COMMENTS
On behalf of the City Manager, the City’s staff involved in development review and administration meets
together as a Development Review Committee (DRC). The DRC generally consists of the City Manager, City
Attorney, City Engineer, Public Works Director, the City Planner, and other outside consultants as needed
from time to time. The comments of the DRC members have been included in this staff report and the
recommended conditions of approval for the project.
City Engineer. The outstanding requirements by the City Engineer and Public Works Department are
included in the recommended conditions of approval. The layout, design and construction of all
subdivisions are subject to the Bluffdale City Engineering Standards and Specifications.
Fire Chief. Fire Chief Roberts approved the plans as submitted. The applicants will be required to receive
approval from the Fire Chief for secondary access prior to the City issuing building permits beyond thirty
(30) residential units.
City Planner. The Planning Division is recommending approval with the recommended conditions.
DRC STAFF RECOMMENDATION FOR BRINGHURST STATION TOWNHOMES SITE PLAN – EDGE HOMES:
DRC Staff recommends that the Planning Commission approve the Bringhurst Station Site Plan Application
subject to the following conditions:
1.
2.

3.
4.
5.
6.
7.

8.

That all requirements of the City Codes, adopted ordinances and the Bringhurst Project Plan are met
and adhered to for each phase Bringhurst Station project.
That all plats within the project comply with the Bluffdale City Engineering Standards and
Specifications and recommendations by the City Engineer and Public Works Department for all
relevant construction and plat drawings.
That all construction drawings be approved and stamped by the City Engineer prior to any
construction of improvements.
That the project adheres to all requirements of the International Fire Code.
That only thirty (30) residential dwelling units are eligible for a building permit until a second access
is provided and approved by the Fire Chief.
That all private open space, landscaping, parking areas and alleys be owned and maintained by the
Homeowners Association (HOA) and improvements installed as per approved plan.
That all public park strip and right-of-way areas be maintained by the HOA or adjacent property
owner including the installation and maintenance of street trees according to the Bringhurst Station
Project Plan.
That all architectural designs and details for all dwelling types including building materials, colors,
and elevations will be submitted for review and approval to the Bringhurst Station Architectural
Review Committee (BSARC) prior to any building permit being issued. A letter with approval of the
BSARC shall be submitted with a building permit application.
Site Plan Application – Bringhurst Station – Edge Homes
January 8, 2020, Planning Commission Meeting
Page 2

9.

That the applicant submits a landscaping phasing plan that illustrates minimum landscaping
installation around each constructed building and associated parkstrip landscaping, prior to the final
inspection for any constructed building or dwelling in the project. The City Planner shall approve the
landscaping phasing plan and no certificate of occupancies for any structure shall be issued unless
all landscaping is installed pursuant to the approved landscaping and phasing plan.

sw

A. EXAMINATION: BEFORE SUBMITTING A BID, EACH CONTRACTOR SHALL CAREFULLY EXAMINE THE CONTRACT
DOCUMENTS; SHALL VISIT THE SITE OF THE WORK; SHALL FULLY INFORM THEMSELVES AS TO ALL EXISTING
CONDITIONS AND LIMITATIONS; AND SHALL INCLUDE IN THE BID THE COST OF ALL ITEMS REQUIRED BY THE
CONTRACT DOCUMENTS ARE AT A VARIANCE WITH THE APPLICABLE LAWS, BUILDING CODES, RULES,
REGULATIONS, OR CONTAIN OBVIOUS ERRONEOUS OR UNCOORDINATED INFORMATION, THE CONTRACTOR
SHALL PROMPTLY NOTIFY THE PROJECT REPRESENTATIVE AND THE NECESSARY CHANGES SHALL BE
ACCOMPLISHED BY ADDENDUM.

B. THE TRADE CONTRACTOR SHALL HAVE FOURTEEN (14) DAYS IN WHICH TO CORRECT ANY WARRANTY ISSUES.
THE OWNER HAS THE RIGHT TO CORRECT ANY ISSUES THAT HAVE NOT BEEN CORRECTED BY THE RESPONSIBLE
TRADE CONTRACTOR WITHIN THE FOURTEEN (14) DAY PERIOD. TRADE CONTRACTOR WILL BE BACK-CHARGED
AT THE RATE OF $50.00 PER MAN HOUR (MINIMUM CHARGE OF $140.00), PLUS THE COST OF ANY MATERIALS
AND EQUIPMENT, FOR WARRANTY ISSUES THE OWNER OR AGENTS CORRECT. IN ADDITION, ANY ISSUE NOT
CORRECTED BY THE TRADE CONTRACTOR WITHIN FOURTEEN (14) DAYS MAY RESULT IN A FINE OF $100 PER
DAY STARTING ON DAY 15, AND CONTINUING DAILY UNTIL THE ISSUE HAS BEEN CORRECTED. THE OWNER
MAY ALSO WITHHOLD CHECKS UNTIL ANY OUTSTANDING (PAST 14 DAYS) WARRANTY ISSUES HAVE BEEN
COMPLETED.

***THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT PROVIDES QUANTITY AND FOOTAGE INFORMATION AS AN AIDE TO THE
BIDDING LANDSCAPER. QUANTITIES, SPECIES, AND SPECIFICATIONS HAVE BEEN SUBMITTED AND
APPROVED BY THE MUNICIPALITIES – ALL SUBSTITUTIONS AND LESSER QUANTITIES MUST BE
APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL
NOT BE PAID FOR BY EDGE HOMES OR THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT

SITE MATERIALS (TOTAL SITE)
LAWN AREA : TOTAL SITE: 247,994 SQ. FT.
LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
MULCHED AREA : TOTAL SITE: 50,345 SQ.FT. (466.16 CU. YDS.)
MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.
DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS: TOTAL SITE: 36,928 SQ.FT. (341.9 CU. YDS)
DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP
IRRIGATION.

BIDDING LANDSCAPER. QUANTITIES, SPECIES, AND SPECIFICATIONS HAVE BEEN SUBMITTED AND
APPROVED BY THE MUNICIPALITIES – ALL SUBSTITUTIONS AND LESSER QUANTITIES MUST BE
APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL
NOT BE PAID FOR BY EDGE HOMES OR THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT

BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

W

DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP
IRRIGATION.

(1)PC

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

PV'S

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

***THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT PROVIDES QUANTITY AND FOOTAGE INFORMATION AS AN AIDE TO THE
BIDDING LANDSCAPER. QUANTITIES, SPECIES, AND SPECIFICATIONS HAVE BEEN SUBMITTED AND
APPROVED BY THE MUNICIPALITIES – ALL SUBSTITUTIONS AND LESSER QUANTITIES MUST BE
APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL
NOT BE PAID FOR BY EDGE HOMES OR THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT

LAWN AREA : TOTAL SITE: 247,994 SQ. FT.

SS

CA'B

W

SW

SW

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP

INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

***THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT PROVIDES QUANTITY AND FOOTAGE INFORMATION AS AN AIDE TO THE
BIDDING LANDSCAPER. QUANTITIES, SPECIES, AND SPECIFICATIONS HAVE BEEN SUBMITTED AND
APPROVED BY THE MUNICIPALITIES – ALL SUBSTITUTIONS AND LESSER QUANTITIES MUST BE
APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL
NOT BE PAID FOR BY EDGE HOMES OR THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP
IRRIGATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

BIDDING LANDSCAPER. QUANTITIES, SPECIES, AND SPECIFICATIONS HAVE BEEN SUBMITTED AND
APPROVED BY THE MUNICIPALITIES – ALL SUBSTITUTIONS AND LESSER QUANTITIES MUST BE
APPROVED BY THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND EDGE HOMES MANAGEMENT. IT IS ENTIRELY THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL
NOT BE PAID FOR BY EDGE HOMES OR THE LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

RESPONSIBILITY OF THE BIDDERS TO CALCULATE THE MATERIAL BASED ON THE GRADE CONTOURS,
INCLUDING ALL LANDSCAPING RETAINING WALLS INDICATED ON THE CIVIL AS WELL AS THE
LANDSCAPING DRAWINGS. THESE CIVIL, LANDSCAPING, AND IRRIGATION DRAWING WILL NEED TO BE
REVIEWED PRIOR TO SUBMITTING A BID FOR THE PROJECT. ANY SHORTAGES OF MATERIALS WILL

LAWN AREAS SHALL BE SOD. NEW TURF AREAS TO BE SODDED WITH 18% "BLUESTAR KENTUCKY
BLUEGRASS, 19% "MARQUIS" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17% "NEWPORT" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 17%
"TOUCHDOWN" KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS, 16% "apm PERENNIAL RYEGRASS, 13% "ACCENT PERENNIAL
RYEGRASS OR APPROVED EQUAL AT A RATE OF 220 LBS. PER ACRE. FINE LEVEL ALL AREAS PRIOR TO
LAYING SOD.
ALL LAWN AREAS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH 100% COVERAGE BY POP-UP SPRAY HEADS AND GEAR-DRIVEN
ROTORS. ALL DECIDUOUS AND CONIFER TREES PLANTED WITHIN SOD AREAS SHALL HAVE A FOUR FOOT(4')
DIAMETER TREE RING COVERED WITH MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES. SUBMIT
SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.

MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.

MULCHED AREA : TOTAL SITE: 50,345 SQ.FT. (466.16 CU. YDS.)
MULCHED AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE TREE RINGS AROUND DECIDUOUS TREES WITHIN THE LAWN AREAS.
MULCH SHALL BE APPLIED TO A DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") AT THE OUTER EDGE OF TREE RINGS,
TAPERING DOWN TO THE TREE TRUNK, AND SHALL BE MEDIUM CHUNK BARK MULCH, NO SHREDDED FINES.
SUBMIT SAMPLES TO BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN MULCH AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP IRRIGATION.
DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS: TOTAL SITE: 36,928 SQ.FT. (341.9 CU. YDS)
DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL INCLUDE THE PLANTER BEDS AS SHOWN. PLANTER BEDS SHALL BE
COMPLETELY FINISH-COVERED WITH TWO TO FOUR INCH (2"-4") GRAY COLOR COBBLE ROCK. APPLY
DECORATIVE ROCK TO A MINIMUM DEPTH OF THREE INCHES (3") OVER ENTIRE AREA. SUBMIT SAMPLES TO
BE APPROVED BY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT AND OWNER BEFORE INSTALLATION.
ALL TREES AND SHRUBS WITHIN DECORATIVE ROCK AREAS SHALL BE WATERED WITH POINT-SOURCE DRIP
IRRIGATION.

DRC STAFF REPORT
02 January 2020

To:
City of Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Jennifer Robison, Senior Planner, on behalf of the DRC
Re:

Day Ranch Townhomes Phase 2 –Preliminary/Final Subdivision Application
Application No.: 2019-39
Applicant(s):
Woodside Homes
Project Location: Approximately 15200 South Harmon Day Drive
General Plan:
Mixed Use
Zoning:
MU – Mixed Use* (as modified by the Day Ranch Development Agreement specific
provisions, as amended)
Acreage:
13.15 acres
Units:
219 townhome units
Request:
Approval of the Day Ranch Townhomes Phase 2 Preliminary/Final Subdivision Day
Ranch Townhomes including associated private streets and common areas within the
Day Ranch project.

SUMMARY
Subject to the DRC staff’s recommendations, the proposed applications meet the City requirements for Site
Plan and Preliminary and Final Plat approval as outlined in the Bluffdale City Subdivision Ordinance, the
Mixed Use zoning district ordinance, and the contractual requirements of the Day Ranch Development
Agreement (DA) and Project Plan (PP). The Planning Commission approved the overall site plan on December
6, 2017.
ANALYSIS
Anticipated Uses, Layout and Access. The overall Day Ranch Townhomes Site Plan provides for 488
townhome units. There are 3 story rear-alley and 2 story rear-alley loaded units and front-loaded
townhome units. All rear-alley units are accessed from a 20’ wide private alley and alleys are accessed
from private streets. All streets and alleys will be owned and maintained by a Homeowners Association
(HOA). All units are oriented to the private street with internal sidewalks and connecting to each individual
unit for pedestrian connectivity. The main access will be from the public street of Harmon Day Drive on the
east.
Adequate Public Facilities and Infrastructure. All adequate public facilities exist or will be installed with the
project construction for this final phase of the project.
Preliminary and Final Subdivision Application – Day Ranch Townhomes Phase 2
January 8, 2020, Planning Commission Meeting
Page 1

Setbacks. The minimum setbacks for rear-alley residential units are provided in the PP. The front setback is
a minimum of 10 feet, side setback is a minimum of 5 feet, and rear setback is a minimum of 5 feet. The
rear setback from the alley to the building structure is a minimum of 5 feet. There is a minimum of 10 feet
required between structures. Each structure for Day Ranch shall comply with all required setbacks.
ROW, Open Space and Landscaping. The site does contain some private HOA controlled private open space to
be landscaped in compliance with the overall site plan approval. The PP requires maintenance of open spaces for
this plat as follows:
City 10-foot Asphalt Trail
 The trail running the length of Harmon Day Drive on the west and south sides shall be
maintained by the City including the removal of snow.
Concrete Sidewalk Snow Removal
 Snow removal shall be done per the following:
• An HOA shall have responsibility for maintenance north of the roundabout.
Parkway and Extended Easement Landscaping
 North of the roundabout, the parkways and the extended landscaped trail park dedication
shall be installed by the home builder and maintained by an HOA.
 Within the roundabout, parkway and center circle landscaping shall be installed and
maintained by the City.
Raised Center Median
 The raised center median landscaping shall be maintained by the City.
Architecture and Elevations. The Day Ranch Design Review Committee (ARC) is authorized by the DA and PP
and has provided the approval for design and architectural elements of the overall project. Renderings of the
style, types, materials, colors and amenities were approved by the Planning Commission with the overall site
plan.
Fencing. The PP requires thematic fencing throughout the Day Ranch project which should be installed as
required with each plat.
City Construction Standards Compliance. All plats and phases of this Day Ranch project are required to meet
all the City construction standards for street lights, fire hydrants, water lines, pavement, concrete and any
other public infrastructure improvements required to be installed with the project.
DRC REVIEW AND COMMENTS
On behalf of the City Manager, the City’s staff involved in development review and administration meets
together as a Development Review Committee (DRC). The DRC generally consists of the City Manager, City
Attorney, City Engineer, Public Works Superintendents, the City Planner, and other outside consultants as
needed from time to time. The comments of the DRC members have been included in this staff report and
the recommended conditions of approval for the project.
City Engineer. There are no outstanding requirements by the City Engineer and Public Works Department. The
layout, design and construction of all subdivisions are subject to the Bluffdale City Engineering Standards and
Specifications. These requirements are included in the recommended conditions of approval.
Fire Chief. Fire Chief Roberts approved the plans as submitted.
City Planner. The Planning Division is recommending approval with the recommended conditions.
Preliminary and Final Subdivision Application – Day Ranch Townhomes Phase 2
January 8, 2020, Planning Commission Meeting
Page 2

DRC STAFF RECOMMENDATION FOR DAY RANCH TOWNHOMES PHASE 2 PRELIMINARY AND FINAL
SUBDIVISION APPLICATIONS:
DRC Staff recommends that the Planning Commission approve the Day Ranch Townhomes Phase 2
Preliminary and Final Subdivision Application subject to the following conditions:
1. That all requirements of the City Codes, adopted ordinances, Day Ranch Development Agreement
and Project Plan are met and adhered to for each phase of Day Ranch Project.
2. That all plats within the project comply with the Bluffdale City Engineering Standards and
Specifications and recommendations by the City Engineer and Public Works Department for all
relevant construction and plat drawings prior to the plat recording.
3. That the project adheres to all requirements of the International Fire Code.
4. That all public park strips, landscaping, irrigation and maintenance adjacent to the project is the
responsibility of the Homeowners Association (HOA) as outlined in the PP.
5. That all private open space, landscaping, parking areas and alleys be owned and maintained by the
HOA.
6. That all building permit submittals for townhomes have written or stamped approval from the ARC
prior to being submitted to the City for compliance with elevations, materials and finishes as
required by the overall site plan approval.
7. The thematic fencing as required by the PP be installed with each plat by the applicants or home
builder.

LIMITS OF WATER EASEMENT (CITY OF BLUFFDALE)

1.
ALL PUBLIC & PRIVATE ROADS ARE HEREBY DEDICATED AS UTILITY EASEMENTS
FOR WATER, STORMWATER, PRESSURIZED IRRIGATION, AND ALL OTHER CITY
UTILITIES, IN FAVOR OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE AND OTHER UTILITY COMPANIES AS
WELL AS PUBLIC UTILITY EASEMENTS.
2.
SHALLOW SEWER DEPTH! CONTRACTORS SHALL VERIFY SEWER LATERAL DEPTH
AND SET FOUNDATION ELEVATION TO PROVIDE ADEQUATE FALL INTO SEWER
LATERAL. BUILDINGS WITH A BASEMENT MAY NOT HAVE SEWER SERVICE AVAILABLE
FOR BASEMENT.

1.

FOR WATER, STORMWATER, PRESSURIZED IRRIGATION, AND ALL OTHER CITY
UTILITIES, IN FAVOR OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE AND OTHER UTILITY COMPANIES AS
WELL AS PUBLIC UTILITY EASEMENTS.

AND SET FOUNDATION ELEVATION TO PROVIDE ADEQUATE FALL INTO SEWER
LATERAL. BUILDINGS WITH A BASEMENT MAY NOT HAVE SEWER SERVICE AVAILABLE
FOR BASEMENT.

Wednesday, December 11, 2019
Notice is hereby given that the Bluffdale City Council and the Bluffdale City Planning Commission will hold a special joint meeting
Wednesday, December 11, 2019, at the Bluffdale City Hall, 2222 West 14400 South, Bluffdale, Utah, scheduled to begin promptly at
4:30 p.m. or as soon thereafter as possible. Notice is further given that access to this meeting by the Mayor and or City Council may
be by electronic means via telephonic conference call.

BLUFFDALE CITY COUNCIL AND PLANNING COMMISSION JOINT BUSINESS MEETING 4:30 P.M.
1. Roll Call
2. WORK SESSION - Economic Development
3. Closed meeting pursuant to Utah Code § 52-4-205(1) to discuss the character, professional competence, or
health of an individual, collective bargaining, pending or imminent litigation, strategies to discuss real
property acquisition, including any form of a water right or water shares, security issues, or any alleged
criminal misconduct (if needed).
4. Adjournment
Dated: December 6, 2019
I HEREBY CERTIFY THAT THE FOREGOING NOTICE AND AGENDA WAS FAXED TO THE SOUTH VALLEY JOURNAL, THE SALT LAKE
TRIBUNE, AND THE DESERET MORNING NEWS; POSTED AT THE BLUFFDALE CITY HALL, EMAILED OR DELIVERED TO EACH MEMBER OF
THE BLUFFDALE CITY COUNCIL; ON THE CITY’S WEBSITE AT WWW.BLUFFDALE.COM AND ON THE PUBLIC MEETING NOTICE WEBSITE,
WWW.PMN.UTAH.GOV

Wendy L. Deppe, CMC
City Recorder
In compliance with the American with Disabilities Act, individuals needing assistance or other services or accommodation for this
meeting should contact Bluffdale City Hall at least 24 hours in advance of this meeting at 801-254-2200. TTY 7-1-1.

BLUFFDALE CITY COUNCIL
MEETING AGENDA
Wednesday, December 11, 2019
Notice is hereby given that the Bluffdale City Council will hold a meeting Wednesday, December 11, 2019, at the Bluffdale City Hall,
2222 West 14400 South, Bluffdale, Utah, scheduled to begin promptly at 6:00 p.m. or as soon thereafter as possible. Notice is further
given that access to this meeting by the Mayor and or City Council may be by electronic means via telephonic conference call.

WORK SESSION 6:00 P.M. – (The work session is for identifying future items and other council discussion. In
accordance with Utah Code § 52-4-201(2)(a), while the meeting may be open to the public, there will not be any
opportunity for public input during the work session).
1. Roll Call
2. Presentations –
a. Discussion regarding Quail Ridge 1 Subdivision, presenter, Andrade Christensen.
b. Discussion regarding Water, Stormwater, Roads, Transportation Master Plan, staff presenter, Michael Fazio.
3. Discussion relating to being a Sanctuary City for the Unborn, presenter, Ty Nielsen.
4. Council Discussion
REGULAR BUSINESS MEETING 7:00 P.M.
1. Roll Call, Invocation, Pledge of Allegiance*
2. PRESENTATION – Recognition of Travis Higby, Ty Nielsen and Boyd Preece for their dedicated service to the
Bluffdale City Council, Mayor Derk Timothy.
3. PUBLIC FORUM – (4-minute maximum per person to bring items not already on the agenda before the
Council. Participants are encouraged to submit a written statement (1 copy) for items that are complex or
that may require more than 4 minutes to present).
4. CONSENT AGENDA –
4.1 Approval of the November 13, 2019, Special City Council Meeting Minutes (Economic Development).
4.2 Approval of the November 13, 2019, City Council, LBA & RDA Combined Meeting Minutes.
4.3 Approval of the November 19, 2019, City Council Board of Canvassers Meeting Minutes.

4.4 Approval of a resolution reappointing Planning Commission Members.
4.5 Approval of a resolution of the Bluffdale City Council authorizing the City Manager to enter into
agreements awarding a contract for the Zone 1W 4MG Water Tank.
4.6 Approval of a resolution repealing Resolution No. 2019-41, and authorizing execution of an Interlocal
Cooperation Agreement with Jordan School District regarding impact fees, joint use of facilities, and a dog
park.
4.7 Approval of an ordinance amending Chapter 2-2: “Historic Preservation Commission” to set forth
commission duties, standards for additions to historic buildings, and other related administrative updates.
5. Consideration and vote on a resolution of the Bluffdale City Council regarding being a Sanctuary City for the
Unborn, staff presenter, Vaughn Pickell.
6. PUBLIC HEARING – Consideration and vote on an ordinance vacating a portion of 15000 South, a public
street, located at approximately 3235 West (Castle Valley Drive), staff presenter, Vaughn Pickell.
7. CONTINUED FROM NOVEMBER 13, 2019 – Consideration and vote on an ordinance amending Section 7-2-4
of the Bluffdale City Code related to parking during a snowstorm, including parking on alternate sides of the
street, staff presenter, Shane Paddock.
8. Consideration and vote on a resolution stating a goal of achieving 100 percent of the annual electric energy
supply for participating customers from a renewable energy resource by 2030, staff presenter, Vaughn Pickell.
9. Mayor’s Report
10. City Manager Report and Discussion
11. Closed meeting pursuant to Utah Code § 52-4-205(1) to discuss the character, professional competence, or
health of an individual, collective bargaining, pending or imminent litigation, strategies to discuss real
property acquisition, including any form of a water right or water shares, security issues, or any alleged
criminal misconduct (if needed).
12. Adjournment
Dated: December 6, 2019
I HEREBY CERTIFY THAT THE FOREGOING NOTICE AND AGENDA WAS FAXED TO THE SOUTH VALLEY JOURNAL, THE SALT LAKE TRIBUNE, AND
THE DESERET MORNING NEWS; POSTED AT THE BLUFFDALE CITY HALL, EMAILED OR DELIVERED TO EACH MEMBER OF THE BLUFFDALE CITY
COUNCIL; ON THE CITY’S WEBSITE AT WWW.BLUFFDALE.COM AND ON THE PUBLIC MEETING NOTICE WEBSITE, WWW.PMN.UTAH.GOV

Wendy L. Deppe, CMC
City Recorder
In compliance with the American with Disabilities Act, individuals needing assistance or other services or accommodation for this meeting
should contact Bluffdale City Hall at least 24 hours in advance of this meeting at 801-254-2200. TTY 7-1-1. *Contact the City Recorder if you
desire to give the Invocation or lead the Pledge of Allegiance.
